7. NMPC – Percentage of Calls Answered by a Representative in 30 seconds 
 
7.1. Definition 
 
a. Per the NMPC JP, this metric measures the percentage of all inbound Customer 
Service and Collections calls to Niagara Mohawk or its agents, regardless of location, 
that are answered within 30 seconds.   
 
7.2. Data Inputs 
 
a. The following Company systems are used as data sources: Cisco Unified Intelligence 
Center (CUIC),  Integrated Voice Response ("IVR"), internal sources, 3rd party outage 
system, and external sources. 
 
b. All calls received 24/7 are included in the metric. 
 
7.3. How calculated 
 
a. Annual performance is based on 12 months’ data.  For the year, the measure is the 
number of inbound Customer Service and Collections calls answered by a 
representative within 30 seconds as a percentage of the total calls answered during 
the year (note that while NMPC’s metric is based on year-end data, KEDNY and 
KEDLI’s metric is based on the average of the 12 months’ scores). 
 
(Total calls answered within 30 seconds by a representative (internal and vendor) / 
(Number of total calls (both internal and vendor) requesting a representative – all calls 
abandoned) x 100

7.4. Exclusions 
a. The metric excludes calls answered by any current or future IVR applications such as
Niagara Mohawk’s Customer Connection, icCallMe or OnCall applications.
b. Abandoned calls have historically been excluded from the metric.
7.5. Process 
a. Customer calls are recorded and time stamped as they are received by the call center.
Calls are initially routed to the IVR for self-service.
b. Within the IVR, customers can select to speak to a call center representative.  When this
selection is made, the call is recorded as a call requesting a call center representative.
The call is placed in the queue and a time counter begins within the switch to measure
the waiting time until the call center representative answers the call.  The call leaves the
queue and the time counter stops when the call is transferred to an available call center
representative.
c. Raw data with call statistics (e.g., Offered calls, Automatic Call Distribution (“ACD”), Calls
Answered in 30 Seconds, and Service Level) are maintained in the IVR and CUIC and
Calabrio call management systems.
d. The Service Level (i.e., the performance level) is calculated as follows:
(Total answered within 30 seconds / ACD) x 100
e. The team has a daily compiled Power BI Regulatory dashboard that tracks the metrics
performance.
f.
Monthly metrics are prepared in the related PSC file which also contains the required
calculations, up on completion it is compared to the Regulatory dashboard and raw data
sources for errors, a final review will then be requested.
g. The reviewer will cross-check the metrics for any anomalies in comparison to the
expected results, and checked-marked for approval.

8. NMPC Annual Reporting for Missed Appointments
As per the NMPC rate case order issued on January 20, 2022, Niagara Mohawk’s CSPI will 
include the numbers and total dollars paid to customers for missed appointments.  On an 
annual basis, the NY Revenue Requirements group sends a request to AMO provide the 
monthly data for the number and total dollar amounts paid to customers for missed 
appointments for the CY.  AMO coordinates with Dispatch to complete the request.   
8.1. Data Source 
a. Data source comes directly from the monthly data gathered for the Appointments Kept
21.3 process for NMPC steps a through c.
8.2. Process 
a. Dispatch Supervisor runs a query of sharepoint of the missed appointments for UNY
where the credit warranted flag is true for the prior calendar year.
b. Dispatch Supervisors exports the query to excel and reviews it for accuracy and then
sends it to the AMO Analyst.
c. AMO Analyst modifies the missed appointment excel file to identify the true credit
warranted flag and add the $30 credit and provide the total volume and total credits.
i.
Adds column A and labels it Credit Required
ii.
Adds formula =IF(OR([@[Credit Warranted]] = TRUE, [@[Credit Warranted]] =
"Yes"), 30, 0) to the rows under column A
iii.
Adds the Total Credit Amount and Total Credits Applied at the bottom of the
spreadsheet.  The sum formula is used for the volume and the total credits are
manually added by multiplying the total volume by the $30 credit.
d. AMO Analyst sends the completed report back to NY Revenue Requirements and
puts Dispatch on copy.
e. Dispatch Manager and AMO Manager has retained the Report Owner/Reviewer roles
and has delegated Report Preparation roles to a member of their team (listed below)
i.
UNY:
a) Report Owners / Reviewers: Manager, Dispatch and Analyst, AMO
b) Report Preparers: Lead Supervisor, Dispatch and Analyst, AMO

9. NMPC SQ Report 
 
 
9.1. Annual Report 
 
a. Niagara Mohawk is required to submit annual performance results, together with 
supporting data and reports, to the Secretary within three months after the conclusion of 
each full calendar year. For calendar years 2022-2024, the annual reports will reflect the 
migration from the prior Service Quality Assurance Program metrics to the new  customer 
service PI titles and metrics.  The annual customer service PI reports for calendar years 
2022-2024 will reflect the change from fixed dollar negative revenue adjustments to 
equivalent basis points of 10 basis points per metric for calendar 2022, and 12 basis 
points per metric for calendar years 2023-2024.  The NMPC annual customer service PI 
reports will be filed in both this rate case and Case 15-M-0566.   
 
b. The report is to include: a description of the service quality measures, the method for 
calculating performance, the results for the period, supporting calculations of annual 
results in spreadsheet format, and a narrative overall assessment of customer service 
performance during that calendar year. NMPC’s annual customer service PI reports will 
include the number and total dollar amounts paid to customers for missed appointments.  
The annual customer service PI report will include the dollar value of a combined basis 
point for the electric and gas businesses for the applicable calendar year. 
 
c. A sample annual report is included in Appendix 3 to this manual.

d. If NMPC believes in any year that its inability to meet any of the established SQ targets

Commission for relief from any associated negative revenue adjustment. 
 
9.2.  Process for Filing Annual Report and Independent Review 
 
 
a. Annual Report – By the first week of March, Revenue Requirements contacts the 
responsible Business Unit to obtain the year-end data and annual narrative for 
each of the SQ metrics. 
 
b. The responsible Business Unit compiles the data and annual narrative for each of 
the SQ metrics (as described above) and reviews the data and narrative for 
accuracy and completeness using the Procedure Document and applicable metric 
calculation instructions saved to the SharePoint site as references.   
 
c. By the third week of March, the responsible Business Unit provides the data and 
annual narrative to Revenue Requirements. 
 
d. Revenue Requirements prepares the annual SQ report and reviews same for 
accuracy and completeness using the Procedure Document and applicable metric 
calculation instructions saved to the SharePoint site as references.     
 
e. Revenue Requirements sends the completed annual SQ report to the Business 
Units to review. 
 
f. The Business Units review the completed annual SQ report and provide their sign-
off to Revenue Requirements for the report to be filed. 
 
g. Once the Business Units’ sign-off is obtained, Revenue Requirements sends the 
following e-mail, with the highlighted text filled in, to the responsible Business Unit 
metric contacts and Director of NY Legal: 
 
a. “I have completed the review of the Annual yyyy SQ report for NMPC.  I 
reached out to the business leads for each metric for their data.  I then checked 
their formulas, ensured the SQ report metrics that overlapped with the CSPI

11. KEDNY – PSC Complaint Rate
11.1. 
Definition 
a. The PSC Complaint Rate is the annual average monthly rate of complaints per
100,000 customers.
b. Per the KEDNY/KEDLI JP, a PSC complaint is initiated with a dispute being filed by,
or on behalf of, a customer with the Commission. The basis of the complaint must be
Exhibit ___ (CP-1R) 
Page 70 of 114


--- Page 135 ---

CUSTOMER 
SPONSOR: 
HELEN BURT 
one within KEDNY’s control.  Only “charged” complaints are included in the PSC 
Complaint Rate metric. 
c. In addition, complaints brought by energy service companies (“ESCOs”) on behalf of
their customers over which KEDNY has control (e.g., meter issues) will be included in
the PSC Complaint Rate metric, provided the ESCO follows the Commission’s
designated complaint procedures and provides KEDNY with a reasonable opportunity
to address the matter.  ESCO complaints on behalf of customers over which KEDNY
has no control, such as slamming, will not be included in the metric.  For an ESCO to
represent a customer with a complaint, the ESCO must follow the Commission’s
procedures and obtain specific agency agreement.
11.2. 
Data Inputs 
Complaints 
a. A complaint by a customer to the PSC is initially registered by the PSC in the QRS.  A
QRS complaint is not included in the SQ metric.  If KEDNY cannot resolve a QRS
complaint with a customer, it is escalated to a “charged” complaint in the SRS.  At this
stage, the complaint is included in the metric.
b. A complaint will be considered “charged” when a customer, after first having
contacted KEDNY to express dissatisfaction with an action, practice, or conduct of
KEDNY, and having provided KEDNY a reasonable opportunity to address the matter,
contacts the Commission to express dissatisfaction with such action, practice, or
conduct.  A complaint will not be charged if the contact with the Commission is for an
opinion or inquiry.
c. The Company enters all QRS and SRS cases into its Salesforce C3M system and
runs internal reports weekly.
d. DPS Staff enters all cases into the PSC Portal.
Customer Total 
e. The total number of customers should be the unduplicated number of customer
accounts as of December 31st of the previous calendar year.
11.3. 
How Calculated

11.5. 
Process 
 
a. A customer calls the PSC with a complaint.   
 
b. The PSC initiates a complaint record in the PSC Portal and emails the complaint to 
the Company. 
 
c. Regulatory Inquiry Sr. Rep Post 98 or Senior Supervisor, Office of the President 
reviews the complaint, uploads the complaint into the Salesforce C3M System, and 
addresses the complaint.

d. Regulatory Inquiry Sr. Rep Post 98 or Senior Supervisor handles the complaint to 
resolution with the customer, working with other Company organizations, as 
appropriate.  This individual records progress in the Salesforce C3M System and 
submits the results to the PSC. 
 
e. Regulatory Inquiry Sr. Rep Post 98 or Senior Supervisor, Office of the President 
reviews the complaint case record in Salesforce C3M and submits the final case 
record to the PSC through the PSC Portal. 
 
f. The PSC reviews the response from the Company and closes the complaint case, if 
satisfied. 
 
g. If warranted, or Senior Supervisor or Regulatory Inquiry Sr. Rep Post 98 prepares a 
petition to the PSC for a change in the status of a charged complaint. 
 
h. Monthly, the total number of SRS cases entered into the Salesforce C3M System is 
tallied and compared against the total number of SRS cases entered into the PSC 
Portal by the Lead Analyst, Office of the President. 
 
i. By the 1st week of January, the C&I Credit and Collections Manager provides the 
customer count as of 12/31 to Utility Consumer Assistance Specialist III, Office of 
Consumer Services, for use in the upcoming year’s PSC Complaint Rate metric.  
 
j. Lead Analyst extracts the complaint rate for the month.  The Manager, Office of the 
President provides the complaint rate for the month to Revenue Requirements for 
insertion in the annual SQ report.

12.1. 
Definition 
 
a. A measure of the proportion of customer bills that require later adjustment as a result 
of errors by KEDNY, expressed as a percentage of total customer bills. 
 
12.2. 
Data Inputs 
 
a. Total number of bills – all bills rendered to customers. 
 
b. Total number of bills adjusted – all rebills, inclusive of non-Company error rebills. 
 
c. Total non-Company error rebills – non-Company error rebills that are excluded from 
the metric (discussed below). 
 
d. Net adjusted bills – total bills minus non-Company error rebills. 
 
12.3. 
How Calculated 
 
a. The metric is measured monthly and is based on the average of the 12 monthly 
scores. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
12.4. 
Exclusions 
 
a. Per the KEDNY/KEDLI JP, the following are not adjusted bills for purposes of the SQ 
metric: (i) an estimated bill replaced by a bill based on an actual reading; (ii) a 
customer reading replaced with an actual or estimated reading; (iii) a change in a 
customer bill because of switching to or from budget billing; or (iv) changes to correct 
for the responsible billing party (e.g., where bills have been rendered to a prior 
Adjusted Customer Bills

reading.  This is due to system limitations.    
b. Final bills that are re-billed to a different date are excluded from the metric as well.
12.5. 
Process 
a. To obtain the metric components from the CRIS system, Analyst, Billing Operations
performs the following:
a) Total bills rendered from monthly query "Update_Bills_Rendered_Detail"
(all bills rendered from the GAS_BILL table for the calendar month except
those where CDE_TYPE_BILL <> 'UAG' - unaccounted for gas).
b) Total re-bills rendered from monthly query "Update_Bills_Rendered_Detail"
(all bills rendered where CDE_TYPE_BILL in ('REB', REF') - rebill and
refinal).
c) Total conditioners from monthly query "Update_Bills_Adj_Cond_Detail"
which reflects "FINAL BILLS WHICH ARE REBILLED TO A DIFFERENT
FINAL DATE" and "BILLS ORIGINALLY BASED ON AN ESTIMATE AND
SUBSEQUENTLY REBILLED TO AN "ACTUAL" (COMPANY,
CUSTOMER, MANUAL) READ."
b. Analyst, Billing Operations performs the following calculation to arrive at the monthly
adjusted bill figure:
((Total re-bills rendered - Total exclusions (i.e., conditioners)) / Total bills rendered) x
100
c. Analyst, Billing Operations provides the metric result to enter in the annual SQ report.

13. KEDNY – Percentage of Calls Answered
13.1. 
Definition
a. Per the KEDNY/KEDLI JP, this is a measure of the proportion of customer service
calls answered by a KEDNY representative within 30 seconds, expressed as a
percentage of the total calls answered.
13.2. 
Data Inputs 
a. The following Company systems are used as data sources: CUIC, IVR, internal
sources, 3rd party outage system, and external sources.
b. All calls received 24/7 are included in the metric.
13.3. 
How Calculated 
a. The metric is measured monthly and is based on the average of the 12 monthly
scores (note – the calculation is different than NMPC’s calculation).
(Total calls answered within 30 seconds by a call center representative) / Number of
total calls requesting a representative – all calls abandoned) x 100
13.4. 
Exclusions 
a. Abandoned calls have historically been excluded from the metric.
13.5. 
Process 
a. Customer calls are recorded and time stamped as they are received by the call
center.  Calls are initially routed to the IVR for self-service.
Exhibit ___ (CP-1R) 
Page 76 of 114


--- Page 141 ---

b. Within the IVR, customers can select to speak to a call center representative.  When 
this selection is made, the call is recorded as a call requesting a call center 
representative.  The call is placed in the queue and a time counter begins within the 
switch to measure the waiting time until the call center representative answers the 
call.  The call leaves the queue and the time counter stops when the call is transferred 
to an available call center representative. 
 
c. Raw data with call statistics (e.g., Offered calls, Automatic Call Distribution (“ACD”), 
Calls Answered in 30 Seconds, and Service Level) are maintained in the IVR and the 
CUIC and Calabrio call management systems 
 
d. The Service Level (i.e., the performance level) is calculated as follows: 
 
(Total answered within 30 seconds / ACD) x 100 
 
e. The team has a daily compiled Power BI Regulatory dashboard that tracks the metrics 
performance. 
 
f. Monthly metrics are prepared in the related PSC file which also contains the required 
calculations, up on completion it is compared to the Regulatory dashboard and raw 
data sources for errors, a final review will then be requested. 
 
g. The reviewer will cross-check the metrics for any anomalies in comparison to the 
expected results, and checked-marked for approval. 
 
h. Once reviewers check-mark is completed the metric is then uploaded to the Revenue 
Requirements site to be include in the annual Service Quality report; along with the 
monthly back-up source file. 
i. .

14.4. 
Process 
a. The Market Intelligence team receives an automated download of a random
sample of customers who contacted KEDNY and transmits the electronic file to the
survey vendor.  The Market Intelligence team sends the vendor two files each
month with approximately 1,500 randomly-selected customer records each.
b. From each of the two sample files, the survey vendor randomly selects 900
customer records, conducting two mailings each month (for a total of 1,800
customers).  For each mailing, the survey vendor prints and packages the survey,
includes an ID number, and addresses and mails the survey to customers.  A
reminder postcard is also mailed to the customers.
c. The survey vendor processes completed mail surveys received and provides an
electronic data file and data tables that include the results of the satisfaction
question to the Market Intelligence team. The Market Intelligence team then
calculates the year-to-date score by taking an average of the 12 monthly scores in
the year.
d.
The survey vendor consolidates the survey data and sends it back to the Market
Intelligence team.
e. The Company’s survey vendor loads the survey response data file into its SPSS,
tabulates the results, and transmits the data file, Word file, and .ASC file
containing the scores to the Market Intelligence team.
f.
The Market Intelligence team conducts data quality checks by running their own
tabulations on the SPSS file and compares the results against the tabulated
(Word) data sent by the survey vendor. The output of the data quality checks (and
corresponding raw regulatory question data) are saved monthly in an excel
document and saved on the CSPI Sharepoint Site for Backup Data.
g. The Market Intelligence team then publishes the score each month to Revenue
Requirements for insertion in the annual SQ report.

15. KEDNY SQ Report
15.1. 
Annual Report 
a. KEDNY is required to submit annual performance reports to the Secretary to the
Commission within three months after the conclusion of each full calendar year.
b. The annual performance report is to include a description of the service quality
measures, the method for calculating performance, the results for the period,
supporting calculations of annual results in spreadsheet format, and a narrative
overall assessment of customer service performance during the calendar year.
c. If KEDNY believes in any year that its inability to meet any of the established SQ
targets was attributable to force majeure circumstances (causes that are outside
the control of KEDNY and could not be avoided with the exercise of due care), it
may petition the Commission for relief from any associated negative revenue
adjustment.
15.2. 
Process for Filing Annual Report and Independent Review 
a. Annual Report – By the first week of March, Revenue Requirements contacts the
responsible Business Unit to obtain the year-end data and annual narrative for
each of the SQ metrics.
b. The responsible Business Unit compiles the data and annual narrative for each of
the SQ metrics (as described above) and reviews the data and narrative for
accuracy and completeness using the Procedure Document and applicable metric
calculation instructions saved to the SharePoint site as references.

was any significant differences that could indicate a potential issue.”

17. KEDLI – PSC Complaint Rate
17.1. 
Definition 
a. The PSC Complaint Rate is the annual average monthly rate of complaints per
100,000 customers.
b. Per the KEDNY/KEDLI JP, a PSC complaint is initiated with a dispute being filed
by, or on behalf of, a customer with the Commission. The basis of the complaint
must be one within KEDLI’s control.  Only “charged” complaints are included in the
PSC Complaint Rate metric.
c. In addition, complaints brought by energy service companies (“ESCOs”) on behalf
of their customers over which KEDLI has control (e.g., meter issues) will be
included in the PSC Complaint Rate metric, provided the ESCO follows the
Commission’s designated complaint procedures and provides KEDLI with a
reasonable opportunity to address the matter.  ESCO complaints on behalf of
customers over which KEDLI has no control, such as slamming, will not be
included in the metric.  For an ESCO to represent a customer with a complaint, the
ESCO must follow the Commission’s procedures and obtain specific agency
agreement.
17.2. 
Data Inputs 
Complaints

a. A complaint by a customer to the PSC is initially registered by the PSC in the

a QRS complaint with a customer, it is escalated to a “charged” complaint in the
SRS.  At this stage, the complaint is included in the metric.
b. A complaint will be considered “charged” when a customer, after first having
contacted KEDLI to express dissatisfaction with an action, practice, or conduct of
KEDLI, and having provided KEDLI a reasonable opportunity to address the
matter, contacts the Commission to express dissatisfaction with such action,
practice, or conduct.  A complaint will not be charged if the contact with the
Commission is for an opinion or inquiry.
c. The Company enters all QRS and SRS cases into its Salesforce C3M system and
runs internal reports weekly.
d. DPS Staff enters all SRS cases into the PSC Portal.
Customer Total 
e. The total number of customers should be the unduplicated number of customer
accounts as of December 31st of the previous calendar year.
f.
The number of customers shall correspond to the number of accounts (i.e., a
person or entity with two or more accounts shall be counted as two or more
customers).
g. A customer is defined as a single active account recorded in the billing system
regardless of the number of meters.
e. Both residential and non-residential end-use customer accounts are included in
the calculation.
f.
A customer directly metered and billed on a utility’s redistribution or submetered
service class is counted as a single customer.  Individual sub meters and master
metered occupants provided service through a direct metered utility customer are
not counted separately or included in the utility’s customer total.

a. Each month, Analyst, Billing Operations, oversees an automated process that

in electronic format to the Company’s survey vendor.

files to its CAITI system and randomizes the files.     
 
c. The survey vendor randomly draws phone numbers from the file and conducts the 
telephone surveys. 
 
d.  The survey vendor consolidates the survey data, loads the data into SPSS, 
tabulates the results, and sends the files (data file, the PPT file, and the Excel 
Verbatim file) to The Market Intelligence team.  
 
e.  
The Market Intelligence team conducts data quality checks by running their own 
tabulations on the SPSS file and compares the results against the tabulated (PPT) 
data sent by the survey vendor. The output of the data quality checks (and 
corresponding raw regulatory question data) are saved monthly in an excel 
document and saved on the CSPI Sharepoint Site for Backup Data. 
 
f. The Market Intelligence team then publishes the score each month to Revenue 
Requirements for insertion in the annual SQ report.

19. KEDLI – Telephone Answer Response within 30 Seconds 
 
19.1. 
Definition 
 
a. Per the KEDNY/KEDLI JP, this is a measure of the proportion of customer service 
calls answered by a KEDLI representative within 30 seconds, expressed as a 
percentage of the total calls answered. 
 
19.2. 
Data Inputs 
Exhibit ___ (CP-1R) 
Page 88 of 114


--- Page 153 ---
 
Customer Service Quality and Performance Indicator Reports - 
Procedure Document 
Version 7.0  – 3/2/2023 
Page 36 of 61 
 
 
PRINTED COPIES ARE NOT DOCUMENT CONTROLLED.   
© NATIONAL GRID USA SERVICE COMPANY 2023 – ALL RIGHTS RESERVED 
FOR THE LATEST AUTHORIZED VERSION PLEASE REFER TO THE HYPERLINK 
CUSTOMER SERVICE QUALITY AND PERFORMANCE INDICATOR 
REPORTS - PROCEDURE DOCUMENT 
ORIGINATING DEPARTMENT: 
CUSTOMER 
SPONSOR:  
HELEN BURT 
 
 
 
 
a. The following Company systems are used as data sources: Avaya CMS, IVR, 
internal sources, 3rd party outage system, and external sources. 
 
b. All calls received 24/7 are included in the metric. 
 
19.3. 
How calculated 
 
a. The metric is measured monthly and is based on the average of the 12 monthly 
scores (note – the calculation is different than NMPC’s calculation). 
 
(Total calls answered within 30 seconds by a call center representative / number 
of total calls requesting a representative – all calls abandoned) x 100 
 
 
19.4. 
Exclusions 
 
a. Abandoned calls have historically been excluded from the metric.  
 
19.5. 
Process 
 
a. Customer calls are recorded and time stamped as they are received by the call 
center.  Calls are initially routed to the IVR for self-service. 
 
b. Within the IVR, customers can select to speak to a call center representative.  
When this selection is made, the call is recorded as a call requesting a call center 
representative.  The call is placed in the queue and a time counter begins within 
the switch to measure the waiting time until the call center representative answers 
the call.  The call leaves the queue and the time counter stops when the call is 
transferred to an available call center representative.

20. KEDLI – Adjusted Customer Bills 
 
20.1. 
Definition 
 
a. A measure of the proportion of customer bills that require later adjustment as a 
result of errors by KEDLI, expressed as a percentage of total customer bills. 
 
20.2. 
Data Inputs 
 
a. Total number of bills – all bills rendered to customers. 
 
b. Total number of bills adjusted – all rebills, inclusive of non-Company error rebills.

Adjusted Customer Bills 
Negative Revenue Adjustment 
1.50% or less 
$0

20.4. 
Exclusions 
 
a. Per the KEDNY/KEDLI JP, the following are not adjusted bills for purposes of the 
Service Quality: (i) an estimated bill replaced by a bill based on an actual reading; 
(ii) a customer reading replaced with an actual or estimated reading; (iii) a change 
in a customer bill because of switching to or from budget billing; or (iv) changes to 
correct for the responsible billing party (e.g., where bills have been rendered to a 
prior resident, and bills are re-issued to the current  resident).  However, KEDLI 
only excludes from the metric (i) an estimated bill replaced by a bill based on an 
actual reading and (iii) a change in a customer bill because of switching to or from 
budget billing.  This is due to system limitations.   
 
20.5. 
Process

Customer Service Quality and Performance Indicator Reports -

a. To obtain the metric components from the CSS system, Analyst, Billing 
Operations, performs the following: 
 
a) CSS mainframe ad-hoc job 
“CG.CSS.BUSOPS.ADHOC.PROCLIB(MONTH8C)” to produce the total 
bill count, exclusions (i.e., conditioners) and total number of active 
account.  The job creates the “JB108_BILL_COUNTS_ALL.csv” file.  
This file contains the counts of bills rendered, rebills and conditioners for 
KEDLI and NMPC. 
 
b. To calculate the components to yield the metric, the Analyst, Billing Operations  
performs the following: 
 
a) Net Corrected Bills = Number of Rebills – Conditioners 
b) Conditioners is defined as Adjusted Budget Bills + Estimated Replaced 
by Actual 
c) Net Number of Bills is calculated as Number of bills rendered – Net 
Corrected Bills 
 
c. The Analyst, Billing Operations performs the following calculation to arrive at the 
monthly adjusted bill figure: 
 
((Total re-bills rendered - Total exclusions (i.e., conditioners)) / Total bills 
rendered) x 100 
 
d. Billing Operations provides the metric result to Revenue Requirements to enter in 
the annual SQ report. 
 
e. Manager of Billing Operations will review and sign off on the CSPI Worksheet.

21.1. 
Annual Report 
 
a. KEDLI is required to submit annual performance reports to the Secretary to the 
Commission within three months after the conclusion of each full calendar year. 
 
b. The annual performance report is to include a description of the service quality 
measures, the method for calculating performance, the results for the period, 
supporting calculations of annual results in spreadsheet format, and a narrative 
overall assessment of customer service performance during the calendar year. 
 
c. If KEDLI believes in any year that its inability to meet any of the established SQ 
targets was attributable to force majeure circumstances (causes that are outside 
the control of KEDLI and could not be avoided with the exercise of due care), it 
may petition the Commission for relief from any associated negative revenue 
adjustment. 
 
 
 
21.2. 
Process for Filing Annual Report and Independent Review 
 
 
a. Annual Report – By the first week of March, Revenue Requirements contacts the 
responsible Business Unit to obtain the year-end data and annual narrative for 
each of the SQ metrics. 
 
b. The responsible Business Unit compiles the data and annual narrative for each of 
the SQ metrics (as described above) and reviews the data and narrative for 
accuracy and completeness using the Procedure Document and applicable metric 
calculation instructions saved to the SharePoint site as references.   
 
c. By the third week of March, the responsible Business Unit provides the data and 
annual narrative to Revenue Requirements. 
 
d. Revenue Requirements prepares the annual SQ report and reviews same for 
accuracy and completeness using the Procedure Document and applicable metric 
calculation instructions saved to the SharePoint site as references.

22.2. 
Process for Completing, Independently Reviewing, and Submitting Monthly  
PI Report to the DPS Staff 
 
a. The monthly PI metrics should be reported in accordance with the “Customer Service 
Metrics Manual” (revised February 16, 2022).  A copy is attached to the PI Order 
(Appendix 5 to this document).   
 
b. Per page 6 of the PI Order, any departure from the “Customer Service Metrics 
Manual” should be footnoted in the monthly PI report.   
 
c. After reviewing the accuracy and completeness of the metric using the Procedure 
Document and applicable metric calculation instructions saved to the SharePoint site 
as references, the responsible Business Units enter their respective metric(s) for the 
prior month into the  spreadsheet that is stored on the access-controlled SharePoint 
site by the 3rd Monday each month. 
 
d. The responsible Business Unit also uploads the back-up data for their respective 
metric(s) for the prior month to the SharePoint site by the 3rd Monday each month. 
 
e. Revenue Requirements independently reviews the monthly PI Report for 
mathematical accuracy and reasonableness using the Procedure Document and 
applicable metric calculation instructions saved to the SharePoint site as references.  
 
f. The Revenue Requirements Analyst (“Analyst”): 
 
a. Verifies all PI metrics have been entered through the applicable month.  For 
any missing PI metrics, the Analyst will check if sufficient footnotes explaining 
the missing metric(s) were entered. 
 
b. Reviews the previous month’s CSPI Report to ensure that all prior month PI 
metrics have not been altered.  For any altered PI metrics, the Analyst will 
check if sufficient footnotes explaining the changed metric(s) were provided. 
 
c. Checks the accuracy of PI metrics using the Procedure Document and 
applicable metric calculation instructions saved to the SharePoint site as 
references.

The sections that follow highlight differences between NMPC, KEDNY, and KEDLI’s 
reporting of the PI metrics and the “Customer Service Metrics Manual,” as well as 
include other relevant information.  Differences should be footnoted in the monthly PI 
report.   
 
22.3. 
Appointments Kept metric

a. Definitions: 
i. Appointment Made - A schedule meeting between the utility and the customer (or 
customer representative) at the customer's premises in which a date and time or 
time frame is agreed upon by both parties.  The measure only applies to 
appointments where the customer or his or her representative must be present to 
provide access. 
ii. The utility arrives at the previously agreed upon location, date, and time window. 
iii. Appointment Missed - If the utility does not satisfy the terms of the Appointment Kept. 
b. NMPC, KEDNY, and KEDLI will include the following footnotes in the monthly PI 
report: 
 
i. 
The metric includes appointments after normal business hours and on weekends. 
 
An attachment is included listing the appointments that are included in the data 
reported to the PSC and matching them to the Job Types in the Customer Service 
Manual, where applicable.  
 
c. Data Source 
 
i. 
NMPC:  
Dispatch Supervisors will review a Customer Commitment report in Salesforce 
throughout the day to manage the customer appointments.  This is a real-time 
report that shows the status of each customer appointment as well as the 
appointment windows.  The following day the Dispatch Supervisors will review the 
Service Appointments report in Power BI for accuracy.  This report identifies 
missed appointments for each NMPC electric and gas work order generated in 
response to a customer’s request. 
 
ii. 
KEDNY:   
Microstrategy “Appointments” and “Missed Appointments” are manually-generated 
reports.Dispatch enters all missed appointments into the SharePoint report and 
reviews for reason missed.Whether customers will receive a credit will be 
determined based on the reason and selecting YES on the credit warranted field 
for customers eligible for credit. Once the reports are reviewed and reconciled by 
Customer Meter Services (“CMS”) Dispatch, Accounts Management Organization 
(“AMO”) issues service guarantee credits for accounts noted.   
 
iii. 
KEDLI:

Dispatch runs a report in Salesforce/Power BI called the Service Appointments

d. Process 
1) NMPC: 
a. Employee Information 
i. 
Manager, Gas CMS Dispatch UNY 
ii. 
Lead Supervisor, Gas CMS Dispatch UNY 
iii. 
(5) Supervisors, Gas CMS Dispatch UNY 
b. Dispatch Supervisors will review the Service Appointments report in Power 
BI for accuracy 
i. 
For incorrect missed appointments on the Service Appointments 
report, the Dispatch Supervisor will enter time edits in Maximo to apply 
the corrections. 
ii. 
For correct missed appointments on the Service Appointments report, 
the Dispatch Supervisor will enter this data into the Sharepoint Missed 
Appointments report and select Yes in the Credit Warranted field of 
this report.   
c. Accounts Maintenance & Operations (AMO) will review the Sharepoint Missed 
Appointments report and issue the $30 service guarantee credit to the 
customer’s bill account for all accounts that have the Credit Warranted field as 
Yes on this report. 
d. By the 10th day of each month the Lead Supervisor and/or Dispatch Manager 
will run the Service Appointments report in Power BI for the previous month for 
accuracy. 
e. After final review of this report, the following data is entered into the CSPI     
Report folder for the current year on the CSPI Metrics SharePoint site, 1a) 
Appointments Made and 1b) Appointments Kept, which then gives you 1c) 
Percentage of Appointments Kept using the below calculation. 
f. A copy of the Summary tab of the Service Appointments report in Power BI is 
uploaded to the CSPI Report Back-up Data folder for current year for proof of 
the data entered. 
g. Dispatch Manager has retained the Metric Owner / Reviewer roles and has 
delegated Metric Preparation roles to a member of their team (listed below)

22.4. 
Adjusted Bills metric 
 
a. NMPC, KEDNY, and KEDLI will include the following footnotes in the monthly PI

i. 
NMPC and KEDLI:  The metric does not include (i) an estimated bill replaced by a 
bill based on an actual Company reading or (ii) a change in a customer bill 
because of switching to or from budget billing. 
 
ii. 
KEDNY:  The metric does not include (i) an estimated bill replaced by a bill based 
on an actual Company reading and (ii) final bills that are re-billed to a different 
date.  
 
iii. 
NMPC, KEDNY, and KEDLI:  The metric includes adjustments due to theft of 
service, shared meters, ESCO bill adjustments, customer reads replaced by 
Company actuals or estimates, and changes in billing party. 
 
b. The SQ and PI metric for KEDNY and KEDLI should be the same.   
 
c. Process – The data sources and process for compiling the PI metric are the same as 
described above for KEDNY and KEDLI’s Adjusted Bills SQ metric.  NMPC follows 
KEDLI’s process.   
 
d. This item will be entered as 2a, 2b, and 2c in the report. 
 
e. Data Review – Adjusted Bills Metric Review Process – All Regions 
Metric Owners / Reviewer: Manager, System Modeling and Change Requests 
Metric Preparer: Sr. Analysts, Billing Operations 
 
Role Distinctions: 
Metric Owner / Reviewer – Responsible for the timely and accurate update of 
Adjusted Bills report monthly.  The Metric Owner / Reviewer will make all decisions 
related to the collection, storage and compilation of data required to produce the 
metric. 
 
Metric Preparer – Responsible for the collection, storage and compilation of data 
required to produce the Adjusted bill report monthly. 
 
Review Process: 
1. Monthly metrics will be prepared by the appropriate Metric Preparer 
2. Upon completion of the monthly data preparation, the Metric Preparer will alert the 
Metric Owner via email that the monthly preparation is complete and ready for review

consists of ensuring new monthly data is added and spot checking accuracy against

4. Upon completion of the review, the Metric Owner will upload the appropriate
documents to the Central Repository (located here)
*Note: the uploading of the monthly data by the Metric Owner / Reviewer serves as
evidence that they have completed their review process
f.
Department Responsible
i.
Billing Operations
22.5. 
Telephone Answer Response metric
a. NMPC, KEDNY, and KEDLI will include the following footnotes in the monthly PI
report:
i.
NMPC:  Normal call center operating hours are 7:00 a.m.-7:00 p.m. Monday
through Friday.  Emergency calls are answered 24/7.
ii.
KEDNY:  Normal call center operating hours are 8:00 a.m. - 8:00 p.m. Monday
through Friday and 9:00 a.m. - 5:00 p.m. Saturday.  Emergency calls are
answered 24/7.
iii.
KEDLI:  Normal call center operating hours are 8:00 a.m. - 8:00 p.m. Monday
through Friday.  Emergency calls are answered 24/7.
iv.
NMPC, KEDNY, and KEDLI:  All calls received 24/7 are included in the metric.
v.
NMPC, KEDNY, and KEDLI:  Calls that are abandoned in the IVR before a call
center representative can answer are excluded from the calculation. The capability
to differentiate between calls abandoned before or after 30 seconds have elapsed
is not currently available.

c. Data Source 
 
i. 
Number of scheduled and estimated reads:   
 
a. NMPC:  Internal US Wide Meter Reading Percentages spreadsheet (“Meter 
Reads Spreadsheet”) that is generated and distributed via email by Lead 
Analyst, CMS Massachusetts North and Central, the 2nd week of each month.   
 
i. CSS batch processing runs job CN788WPD that integrates the meter 
reading file delivered from the Field Collection System (“FCS”) and 
populates a meter reading statistics report on the CSS mainframe. 
 
ii. This report is run every five billing cycles with an aggregate of each month 
generated at the close of cycle 20. 
 
iii. Report data is then formatted into the spreadsheet. 
 
b. KEDLI:  Internal Mtr Reading Yrly Roll Up spreadsheet that includes all Itron 
Field Collection System (“FCS”) reads, web-based reads, and IVR reads. 
 
c. KEDNY: FCS Route Status report (Report 15) that is then rolled up into the 
NYC Meter Reading Daily Assignment Sheet.

Role Distinctions: 
• Metric Owner / Reviewer – Responsible for the timely and accurate update
of Estimated Bill Reads report monthly.  The Metric Owner / Reviewer will
make all decisions related to the collection, storage and compilation of data
required to produce the metric.
• Metric Preparer – Responsible for the collection, storage and compilation of
data required to produce the Estimated Bill Reads report monthly.
Review Process: 
i.
Monthly metrics will be prepared by the appropriate Metric Preparers
ii.
Upon completion of the monthly data preparation, the Metric Preparer
will alert the Metric Owner via email that the monthly preparation is
complete and ready for review
iii.
Upon receipt of the alert from the Metric Preparer, the Metric Owner
will access the completed monthly report and review the file for
accuracy and anomalies (the review consists of ensuring new monthly
data is added and spot checking accuracy against source reports,
checking for data anomalies/abnormalities and ensuring appropriate
password protections are in place)
iv.
Upon completion of the review, the Metric Owner will upload the
appropriate documents to the Central Repository (located here)
v.
*Note: the uploading of the monthly data by the Metric Owner /
Reviewer serves as evidence that they have completed their review
process

22.7. 
Residential Customer Satisfaction Survey metric
a.
NMPC, KEDNY and KEDLI will include the following footnote in the monthly PI
report:  Metric has one month lag time.
b. Definition
(1) The PI Order directed Staff to convene a collaborative with the utilities, within
90 days of the issuance of the Order, to consider a statewide customer
satisfaction survey or alternatives and to issue a proposal within six months
thereafter.
(2) In the interim, the PI Order directed the utilities to continue their existing
surveys.
(3) The data, calculation, and process for the PI metric are the same as the
customer satisfaction survey data for the SQ metrics.

Three Empire State Plaza 
Albany, NY 12233-1350 
 
   Re: 
Cases 19-E-0378, 19-G-0379 - Proceeding on Motion of the Commission as to the 
Rates, charges, rules and Regulations of New York State Electric & Gas 
Corporation:  Quarterly Credit Card Transaction Report 
 
 
Cases 19-E-0380 and 19-G-0381 –  Proceeding on Motion of the Commission as to 
the Rates, charges, rules and Regulations of Rochester Gas and Electric Corporation: 
Quarterly Credit Card Transaction Report 
 
Dear Secretary Phillips: 
 
In accordance with Appendix JJ of the Order Approving Electric and Gas Rate Plans in Accord 
with Joint Proposal, with Modifications, issued and effective November 19, 2020 in the above 
referenced proceedings, New York State Electric & Gas Corporation (“NYSEG”) and Rochester 
Gas and Electric Corporation (“RG&E”) hereby submit the attached Quarterly Credit Card 
Transaction report, which covers the quarter ending March 31, 2021. 
 
If you have any questions pertaining to this information, please contact me. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Respectfully submitted 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Tracey A. Pelella 
 
Enclosure
